I guess I've dropped some. \n\nMy turnout gear feels a lot looser lately. Same with my station uniform. It just... hangs off me a bit more. Mostly just looking at my waist, or... if my face looks sharp enough. If it doesn't... I don't know. It just kind of throws me off. Makes me feel less... confident, I guess. Less ready to take charge on shift.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to cope with your anxiety about weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I mean, I just... I check the mirror like I said, but... I don't really have any rituals or anything like that. Just... trying to stick to the plan.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to engage in excessive exercise to burn off calories or maintain your weight?\n\nPatient: No... not overwhelming, no. I just... I work out because of my job. You have to be fit to be a firefighter. So I run, do some lifting... but it's just part of the job. I don't... I don't have to do it to burn calories or anything. Just staying in shape.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I've dropped some. \n\nMy turnout gear feels a lot looser lately. Same with my station uniform. It just... hangs off me a bit more. We had a fitness review recently and they... they actually flagged me. Said I was too light for my height and build. \n\nBut... I don't know. Even with them saying that, I still feel like... maybe I could just trim down a little bit more. Just to be safe.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to restrict your food intake even if you weren't worried about being seen as soft by others?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I mean, the food thing is just... it's just what I do now. I don't really think about it like that. I just want to keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to eat as little as you do now if you didn't care about what others think of your appearance?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just... I just eat what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to restrict your food intake if you could maintain your current weight without changing your eating habits?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. It's just... I came in because, well, I started out just trying to eat cleaner. You know, for the lieutenant track. But... now it's just... I'm basically down to black coffee and a protein shake during the day, and then a small dinner. Even on shift days, when I know I'm burning a lot more than that. It's just kind of... what I do now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your main goal with your current eating habits is to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. It's just, if I check the scale and it's up even a pound... like after a big station meal or just extra water... I get this... I don't know, this tight panic. Like I completely blew it. I just don't want people thinking I'm soft, or... out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve with your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Not really an exact number, I guess. It's more just... I don't know. When I look in the mirror before work, I mostly just check if my waist and face look sharp enough. And if they don't... it just kind of messes with me. Like it changes how confident I feel, or... whether I feel command-ready for the shift. So I just try to keep it sharp.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about weight and body shape are unreasonable or excessive?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... not really? It just feels like what I need to do right now. I haven't really thought of it as excessive... it's just how I keep on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would still be checking your weight or body shape as frequently if you didn't have to present yourself in a certain way for your job as a firefighter?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I mean... maybe? I've never really thought about it like that. It's just... it's just what I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your weight and body shape would decrease if you were no longer a firefighter?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Hard to say. I mean... I'm not planning on leaving the department, so... I haven't really pictured it. It's just kind of my routine right now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be as concerned about your weight and body shape if you were in a different profession?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I said, I don't really know. I've only ever done this job, so... it's hard to picture. Probably? I just... I don't really think about doing anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about your weight if you didn't care about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really get what you mean. I guess if I didn't care, then... maybe not? But... I don't know. It's just kind of a weird hypothetical. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your weight and body shape are more because of how you want to see yourself or because of how you think others see you as a firefighter?\n\nPatient: I guess... it's mostly about how I see myself. Like, when I look in the mirror before work... I just sort of focus on whether my waist and my face look sharp enough. And... I don't know, if they don't, it just kind of messes with my confidence for the shift. I just don't feel as... command-ready, I guess. So, yeah. Mostly me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
